How slow is the storagesys.dm_io_virtReads, writes and read and write latency per database file
What is the transaction doingsys.dm_tran_actOpen transactions, with the session and the database they hold resources in
Which locks are heldsys.dm_tEvery lock with its resource type, mode, duration and owning request
What deadlockedsystem ring bufferEvery deadlock graph, already captured, with the victim and the resource
What is the schemasys.o, sys.c, sys., sys.parThe catalog: every object, its columns, its types, its parameters and its constraints
What is a permissionsys.fn_my_The effective permissions of the calling principal on a securable, without impersonation
What is the versionSERVERP, sys.dm_osProduct version, CU level, edition, instance start time
What is an availability groupsys.availabi, sys.dm_hadr_availabReplica role, commit and failover mode, synchronisation state and health
What is a jobmsdb.dbo, msdb.dbo.syAgent jobs, their steps, their last outcome and their history
-- The three-query triage script: what is running, what is waiting, and what is
-- blocking. This is the sequence to follow before anything else.
SELECT  r.session_id,
        r.status,
        r.command,
        r.wait_type,
        r.wait_time,
        r.wait_resource,
        r.blocking_session_id,
        r.cpu_time,
        r.total_elapsed_time,
        s.login_name,
        s.host_name,
        s.program_name,
        t.text            AS BatchText
FROM    sys.dm_exec_requests   AS r
LEFT JOIN sys.dm_exec_sessions AS s ON s.session_id = r.session_id
OUTER APPLY sys.dm_exec_sql_text(r.sql_handle) AS t
WHERE   r.session_id <> @@SPID
  AND   r.database_id = DB_ID()
ORDER BY r.total_elapsed_time DESC;
GO

-- The blockers, from the requests view, with the blocked statement beside it.
SELECT  Blocking.session_id  AS BlockingSessionId,
        b.session_id        AS BlockedSessionId,
        DATEDIFF(second, b.start_time, GETDATE()) AS SecondsBlocked,
        bs.program_name     AS BlockingProgram,
        bst.text            AS BlockingBatch,
        bt.text             AS BlockedBatch
FROM    sys.dm_exec_requests AS b
JOIN    sys.dm_exec_requests AS Blocking
       ON Blocking.session_id = b.blocking_session_id
LEFT JOIN sys.dm_exec_sessions AS bs ON bs.session_id = Blocking.session_id
OUTER APPLY sys.dm_exec_sql_text(Blocking.sql_handle) AS bst
OUTER APPLY sys.dm_exec_sql_text(b.sql_handle)         AS bt
WHERE   b.blocking_session_id <> 0;
GO

-- The locks, which is what to read when the blocking view is not enough.
SELECT  tl.request_session_id,
        tl.resource_type,
        tl.resource_associated_entity_id,
        tl.request_mode,
        tl.request_status,
        tl.request_lock_id,
        tl.lock_owner,
        o.name                AS ObjectName,
        c.name                AS ColumnName
FROM    sys.dm_tran_locks AS tl
LEFT JOIN sys.objects     AS o  ON o.object_id  = tl.resource_associated_entity_id
LEFT JOIN sys.columns     AS c  ON c.object_id  = tl.resource_associated_entity_id
                            AND c.column_id = tl.resource_associated_entity_id
WHERE   tl.request_mode <> N'Shared'    -- or: N'IX' for key locks
ORDER BY tl.request_session_id;
GO

-- What the caller can actually do, without impersonating anything.
SELECT  permission_name, state_desc, permission_source
FROM    sys.fn_my_permissions(N'dbo.Customer', N'OBJECT')
ORDER BY state_desc, permission_name;
GO
-- The DMV catalogue as a query, so that it can be read as data rather than
-- as documentation, and so that a script can assert that the views it depends
-- on exist before it is deployed rather than after. The second column is a
-- one-line purpose per view, which is the form that is useful in an incident.
SELECT  V.ViewName,
        CASE WHEN EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM sys.dm_objects d
                          WHERE d.name = V.ViewName AND d.type = N'S')
             THEN 1 ELSE 0 END AS Exists
FROM  (VALUES (N'dm_exec_query_stats',                 N'Cumulative counters per cached statement'),
             (N'dm_exec_cached_plans',                 N'One row per cached plan, with size and use count'),
             (N'dm_exec_query_plan',                   N'Plan XML for a plan handle'),
             (N'dm_exec_sql_text',                     N'Text for a sql_handle or plan_handle'),
             (N'dm_exec_requests',                     N'One row per request in flight'),
             (N'dm_exec_sessions',                     N'One row per session'),
             (N'dm_exec_query_memory_grants',          N'Memory requested, granted and used per query'),
             (N'dm_os_wait_stats',                     N'Cumulative wait time by type; read as a delta'),
             (N'dm_os_waiting_tasks',                  N'What is waiting right now'),
             (N'dm_os_schedulers',                     N'Scheduler state and runnable task counts'),
             (N'dm_os_sys_memory',                     N'Physical memory and the locked-page state'),
             (N'dm_os_process_memory',                 N'The process memory total'),
             (N'dm_os_memory_clerks',                  N'Memory by clerk'),
             (N'dm_io_virtual_file_stats',             N'Reads, writes and latency per database file'),
             (N'dm_db_index_usage_stats',              N'Seeks, scans, lookups and updates per index'),
             (N'dm_db_index_physical_stats',           N'Fragmentation and page counts per index'),
             (N'dm_db_missing_index_details',          N'Indexes the optimiser wished it had'),
             (N'dm_db_stats_properties',               N'Row count, sample and last update for one statistic'),
             (N'dm_db_log_space_usage',                N'Log size and log_reuse_wait_desc'),
             (N'dm_tran_active_transactions',          N'Open transactions and their age'),
             (N'dm_tran_locks',                        N'Locks held, with resource and mode'),
             (N'dm_tran_version_store',                N'Row versions retained for snapshot readers'),
             (N'dm_hadr_availability_replica_states',  N'Synchronisation state of each replica'),
             (N'dm_xe_session_targets',                N'The captured events of an Extended Events session')) AS V(ViewName, Purpose)
ORDER BY V.ViewName;
GO

-- Running the whole triage set at once, which is the script to put on a wall
-- next to the terminal during an incident. Each block is independent, so a
-- failure in one does not hide the others.
SELECT  r.session_id,
        r.status,
        r.command,
        r.wait_type,
        r.wait_time,
        r.wait_resource,
        r.blocking_session_id,
        r.cpu_time,
        r.total_elapsed_time,
        s.login_name,
        s.host_name,
        s.program_name,
        t.text                                             AS BatchText
FROM    sys.dm_exec_requests      AS r
LEFT JOIN sys.dm_exec_sessions   AS s ON s.session_id = r.session_id
OUTER APPLY sys.dm_exec_sql_text(r.sql_handle) AS t
ORDER BY r.total_elapsed_time DESC;
GO

SELECT  name,
        CAST(value        AS int) AS Configured,
        CAST(value_in_use AS int) AS InUse
FROM    sys.configurations
WHERE   name IN (N'max server memory (MB)', N'max degree of parallelism',
                 N'cost threshold for parallelism', N'max worker threads');
GO

SELECT  name, recovery_model_desc, log_reuse_wait_desc, compatibility_level
FROM    sys.databases
ORDER BY name;
GO
SELECT TOP (10) qs.execution_count, CAST(qs.total_logical_reads / NULLIF(qs.execution_count, 0) AS decimal(18,1)) AS AvgLogicalReads, CAST(qs.total_elapsed_time / NULLIF(qs.execution_count, 0) / 1000 AS decimal(18,1)) AS AvgElapsedMs, SUBSTRING(st.text, 1, 120) AS StatementText FROM sys.dm_exec_query_stats AS qs CROSS APPLY sys.dm_exec_sql_text(qs.sql_handle) AS st ORDER BY qs.total_logical_reads DESC; 
-- The instance-level catalog, which is the other half of A.2's database-level
-- views. Everything on a server rather than in a database, gathered into one
-- shape so that a baseline of an instance can be diffed against a later one.
SELECT  ServerName   = @@SERVERNAME,
        MachineName  = CAST(SERVERPROPERTY('MachineName') AS nvarchar(128)),
        ProductName  = CAST(SERVERPROPERTY('ProductName') AS nvarchar(128)),
        ProductVer   = CAST(SERVERPROPERTY('ProductVersion') AS nvarchar(128)),
        ProductLevel = CAST(SERVERPROPERTY('ProductLevel') AS nvarchar(128)),
        UpdateRef    = CAST(SERVERPROPERTY('ProductUpdateReference') AS nvarchar(128)),
        Edition      = CAST(SERVERPROPERTY('Edition') AS nvarchar(128)),
        EditionID    = CAST(SERVERPROPERTY('EditionID') AS int),
        Collation    = CAST(SERVERPROPERTY('Collation') AS nvarchar(128)),
        NetName      = CAST(SERVERPROPERTY('NetName') AS nvarchar(128)),
        InstalledOn  = CAST(SERVERPROPERTY('InstallDate') AS datetime2(3)),
        IsClustered  = CAST(SERVERPROPERTY('IsClustered') AS int),
        ComputerName = CAST(SERVERPROPERTY('ComputerName') AS nvarchar(128));
GO

-- Logins, their type, their database mappings, and whether they are orphaned.
-- A login with no user in any database is either a service account that was
-- never mapped or a user who left, and both need removing.
SELECT  sp.name                                        AS LoginName,
        sp.type_desc,
        sp.is_disabled,
        sp.is_policy_checked,
        sp.authentication_policy_desc,
        dp.name                                        AS UserName,
        dp.type_desc                                   AS UserType,
        dr.name                                        AS DefaultSchema,
        DATEDIFF(day, sp.password_last_set_time, GETDATE()) AS DaysSincePasswordSet
FROM    sys.server_principals AS sp
LEFT JOIN sys.database_principals AS dp ON dp.sid = sp.sid
LEFT JOIN sys.database_principals AS dr ON dr.sid = sp.sid
                                       AND dr.type = 'S'
ORDER BY sp.name;
GO

-- Roles, their members, and the fixed roles that have been granted to a user
-- rather than to a group, which is the usual reason a principal has more than
-- the permissions its name suggests.
SELECT  RoleName  = r.name,
        MemberName = m.name,
        MemberType = m.type_desc
FROM    sys.database_role_members AS drm
JOIN    sys.database_principals   AS r ON r.principal_id = drm.role_principal_id
JOIN    sys.database_principals   AS m ON m.principal_id = drm.member_principal_id
WHERE   r.is_fixed_role = 0
ORDER BY r.name, m.name;
GO

SELECT  ServerRole = r.name,
        MemberName  = m.name,
        MemberType  = m.type_desc
FROM    sys.server_role_members AS srm
JOIN    sys.server_principals   AS r ON r.principal_id = srm.role_principal_id
JOIN    sys.server_principals   AS m ON m.principal_id = srm.member_principal_id
WHERE   r.is_fixed_role = 0
ORDER BY r.name, m.name;
GO

-- The instance-level DMV set, which is what a server baseline is built from.
SELECT  ConfigurationCount   = (SELECT COUNT(*) FROM sys.configurations),
        DatabaseCount        = (SELECT COUNT(*) FROM sys.databases WHERE database_id > 4),
        LoginCount           = (SELECT COUNT(*) FROM sys.server_principals WHERE type IN ('S','U','G')),
        AgentJobs            = (SELECT COUNT(*) FROM msdb.dbo.sysjobs),
        AgentRunning         = (SELECT COUNT(*) FROM msdb.dbo.sysjobs WHERE enabled = 1),
        XESessions           = (SELECT COUNT(*) FROM sys.server_event_sessions),
        XEActive             = (SELECT COUNT(*) FROM sys.server_event_sessions WHERE is_running = 1),
        AvailabilityGroups   = (SELECT COUNT(*) FROM sys.availability_groups),
        LinkedServers        = (SELECT COUNT(*) FROM sys.servers WHERE is_linked = 1),
        CertifiedEncKeys     = (SELECT COUNT(*) FROM sys.symmetric_keys),
        ModuleAssemblies     = (SELECT COUNT(*) FROM sys.assemblies);
GO

-- Assemblies and credentials, which are the two instance-level objects with
-- the widest privileges and the shortest audit trail.
SELECT  ObjectName = 'Assembly', Name = name,
        Details = permission_set_desc
FROM    sys.assemblies
UNION ALL
SELECT  'Credential', name, credential_type_desc
FROM    sys.credentials
ORDER BY ObjectName, Name;
GO

A.3 Error numbers and what they mean

An error number is a better search term than an error message, because the message text is localised and the number is not. The list below is the subset that appears repeatedly and that the message text alone does not make obvious.

NumberMeaningUsual cause and response
102Incorrect syntax nearA typo, or a statement that is not valid in its position. The rest of the line is in the message text
111CREATE PROCEDURECREATE PROCEDURE must be the first statement in a batchA missing G before the CRE
137Must declare the scalar variableA variable declared in a previous batch, or a name that collides with a column
208Invalid object nameThe object does not exist in the current database context, or the default schema resolves elsewhere. Check with OBJE
213Column name or number of supplied values does not match table or number of values suppliedAn INS without a column list against a changed table
2627Violation of PRIMARY KEY constraintA duplicate key. With IDENTIT on, the cause is usually a re-run of an insert
2601Violation of UNIQUE KEY constraintA duplicate in a unique or primary key
547Violation of CHECK or FOREIGN KEY constraintThe CA block has to distinguish these with ERROR_NUMBER()ERROR_NUMBER()
8152Adding a new instance to an existing always on availability group failedSystem databases, login or permission differences between the instances
8645A timeout occurred while waiting for memory resourcesMemory grants: the request wanted more than the instance would grant. Raise max server memorymax server memory or reduce the query’s demand
1205Deadlock victimRead the graph from system rather than guessing; then reduce the transaction and lock footprint
18456Login failed for userBad credentials, disabled login, or a contained authentication problem. The state and the reason are in the error log
229Permission denied on objectA missing GR, or a DE that overrides one
300View or function is not updatableThe view contains a join, an aggregate, UN or a derived table. Write to the base tables or use INSTEAD OFINSTEAD OF
413Column is invalid for this use of the typeA te, nt or im column in a GROUP BYGROUP BY, JO or comparison. Use varchar(max)varchar(max) or convert
8156Duplicate column in the SEL listA SELECT *SELECT * over a join with duplicate column names
8154The table is fullSpace in the data file. A memory-optimized error 413 is different in cause
1826Duplicate key name in the databaseA named constraint or index that already exists; the deployment is not idempotent
1750Could not create constraintUsually a WITH CHECKWITH CHECK foreign key against existing violating rows
184527 days and 14 hours until the trial expiresDeveloper or Evaluation edition. The database is about to become read-only
3301Uncorrelated subquery is disallowedAn EXI that is not correlated; remove the reference or add one
9829SQL Server 2019 and earlier: string aggregation result would exceedSTRIN truncation. Aggregate in batches or into typed columns
3903The COMMIT TRANSACTION request has no corresponding BEGIN TRANSACTIONA mismatched @@TRANCOUNT@@TRANCOUNT, often from dynamic SQL in a module that opened a transaction
3961The transaction ended in the trigger. The batch has been abortedAn AF trigger raised an error with XACT_ on; the whole transaction is doomed and the batch stopped
916The server principal is not able to access the database under the current security contextA contained database user without access, or a login with no user mapping
3201A restore operation failed: the file path is not validNo MO, or a path that does not exist on the target instance
4305The log in this backup set begins at LSN ..., which is too recent to apply to the databaseA broken or out-of-order log chain. Start again from a contiguous point
4309This backup set contains records that were logged before the indicated LSNA differential applied before its full backup
4310Not a valid backup set: a new differential backup cannot be appliedA differential applied to a database that has already had a later one, or the wrong order
4326Log backup chain has been brokenA log backup was skipped. Point-in-time recovery is not available across the gap
4335The STO value is too early or too lateThe target time does not fall inside the LSN range of the final log backup
3445Cannot back up the log file because there is no current database backupA database in SIM recovery has never had a log backup. Take one before detaching or moving the file
3999Could not process request because the table is in the process of being droppedA schema change racing an open reader
3930The current transaction cannot be committed and cannot support operations that write to the log fileXACT_STATE()XACT_STATE() returned -1: the transaction is doomed and cannot be committed
-- Capturing the full error context. The message, the state, the procedure, the
-- line and the text of the failing statement are the five facts a diagnostic
-- needs, and any of them can be the one that identifies the cause.
BEGIN TRY
    EXEC sys.sp_executesql
         N'UPDATE dbo.SalesOrder SET TotalAmount = TotalAmount * 1.1
           WHERE SalesOrderID = @id;',
         N'@id int', 1001;
END TRY
BEGIN CATCH
    SELECT  ErrorNumber    = ERROR_NUMBER(),
            ErrorSeverity  = ERROR_SEVERITY(),
            ErrorState     = ERROR_STATE(),
            ErrorLine      = ERROR_LINE(),
            ErrorProcedure = ERROR_PROCEDURE(),
            ErrorMessage   = ERROR_MESSAGE(),
            XactState      = XACT_STATE(),
            TranCount      = @@TRANCOUNT;

    -- The transaction state decides what the CATCH block is allowed to do.
    -- -1 means doomed: the transaction must be rolled back and the work redone.
    --  1 means the transaction is still committable and can be committed or
    --     rolled back deliberately.
    IF XACT_STATE() <> 0
        ROLLBACK TRANSACTION;

    THROW;
END CATCH;
GO
-- The diagnostics that pair with the errors: a message number tells you what
-- class of failure it was, these three tell you what the server was doing
-- when it happened.
EXEC sys.xp_readerrorlog 0, 1, N'Error: 1205';   -- deadlock
EXEC sys.xp_readerrorlog 0, 1, N'The log or the differential backup file has been truncated';
GO

-- The last three errors raised in this session, without the handler. The
-- statement must run in its own batch for fn_trace_gettable to have anything
-- to report, which is the documented behaviour and the reason this is a
-- procedure rather than an inline query.
CREATE OR ALTER PROCEDURE dbo.usp_ReportLastErrors
    @Count int = 3
AS
BEGIN
    SET NOCOUNT ON;

    SELECT  ErrorNumber    = ERROR_NUMBER(),
            ErrorSeverity  = ERROR_SEVERITY(),
            ErrorState     = ERROR_STATE(),
            ErrorLine      = ERROR_LINE(),
            ErrorProcedure = ERROR_PROCEDURE(),
            ErrorMessage   = ERROR_MESSAGE(),
            XactState      = XACT_STATE(),
            TranCount      = @@TRANCOUNT;
END;
GO

BEGIN TRY
    -- Three deliberate failures of different classes, to show that one
    -- handler classifies all of them.
    SELECT 1 / 0;                                                    -- 8134
    RAISERROR (N'deliberate', 16, 1);                               -- 50000
    RAISERROR (N'with a message and a state', 18, 42);               -- 50000
END TRY
BEGIN CATCH
    EXEC dbo.usp_ReportLastErrors;
    THROW;
END CATCH;
GO

-- A swallowed error, which is what a CATCH block that logs and returns 1
-- does. The log entry is the only record that the operation failed, and a
-- caller that checks the return code and ignores the log has lost the
-- information entirely.
CREATE TABLE dbo.OperationFailure
(
    FailureID     bigint IDENTITY(1,1) NOT NULL CONSTRAINT PK_OperationFailure PRIMARY KEY,
    FailedAt      datetime2(3) NOT NULL CONSTRAINT DF_OperationFailure_FailedAt DEFAULT (SYSUTCDATETIME()),
    OperationName nvarchar(200) NOT NULL,
    ErrorNumber   int NOT NULL,
    ErrorSeverity int NOT NULL,
    ErrorMessage  nvarchar(2048) NOT NULL,
    LoginName     sysname NOT NULL,
    HostName      sysname NULL
);
GO

BEGIN TRY
    RAISERROR (N'Illustrative failure.', 16, 1);
END TRY
BEGIN CATCH
    INSERT dbo.OperationFailure (OperationName, ErrorNumber, ErrorSeverity, ErrorMessage, LoginName, HostName)
    VALUES (N'usp_Example', ERROR_NUMBER(), ERROR_SEVERITY(), ERROR_MESSAGE(), SUSER_SNAME(), HOST_NAME());
END CATCH;
GO

-- Reading the failure log, which is the artefact that turns a silent
-- operation into an auditable one.
SELECT  FailedAt, OperationName, ErrorNumber, ErrorSeverity, ErrorMessage, LoginName, HostName
FROM    dbo.OperationFailure
ORDER BY FailureID DESC;
GO

-- The same thing in a procedure, which is where a CATCH block actually earns
-- its keep: the caller sees a return code and an output parameter rather than
-- having to parse an error message.
CREATE OR ALTER PROCEDURE dbo.usp_TryAdjustCreditLimit
    @CustomerID  int,
    @NewLimit    decimal(18,2),
    @Success     bit            OUTPUT,
    @ErrorNumber int            OUTPUT,
    @ErrorText   nvarchar(2048) OUTPUT
AS
BEGIN
    SET NOCOUNT ON;
    SET XACT_ABORT ON;

    SET @Success = 0;

    BEGIN TRY
        BEGIN TRANSACTION;

        UPDATE dbo.Customer
        SET    CreditLimit = @NewLimit
        WHERE  CustomerID = @CustomerID;

        IF @@ROWCOUNT = 0
            RAISERROR (N'Customer %I64d does not exist.', 16, 1, @CustomerID);

        COMMIT TRANSACTION;
        SET @Success = 1;
        RETURN 0;
    END TRY
    BEGIN CATCH
        IF XACT_STATE() <> 0
            ROLLBACK TRANSACTION;

        SET @ErrorNumber = ERROR_NUMBER();
        SET @ErrorText   = ERROR_MESSAGE();

        -- Re-raise as a lower severity so the caller can handle it without the
        -- batch being aborted, which RAISERROR with severity 16 would cause
        -- under XACT_ABORT.
        RAISERROR (N'usp_TryAdjustCreditLimit failed: %s', 11, 1, @ErrorText);

        RETURN 1;
    END CATCH;
END;
GO

DECLARE @Ok bit, @Num int, @Txt nvarchar(2048);

EXEC dbo.usp_TryAdjustCreditLimit
     @CustomerID  = 42,
     @NewLimit    = 50000.00,
     @Success     = @Ok OUTPUT,
     @ErrorNumber = @Num OUTPUT,
     @ErrorText   = @Txt OUTPUT;

SELECT Success     = @Ok,
       ErrorNumber = @Num,
       ErrorText   = @Txt;
GO

-- Capturing the state of everything the session did during the failed
-- operation, which is the difference between a report that says what happened
-- and one that says it did not happen.
SELECT  EventUtc    = SYSUTCDATETIME(),
        EventType   = N'CREDIT_LIMIT_ADJUST',
        CustomerId  = 42,
        NewLimit    = CAST(50000.00 AS decimal(18,2)),
        Success     = @Ok,
        ErrorNumber = @Num,
        ErrorText   = @Txt;
GO
-- Turning a set of failures into a table rather than a log, so that the
-- numbers in the error list above are reproducible rather than remembered.
-- The script deliberately produces one failure of each class, catches it, and
-- records it; it is a test harness for the CATCH block, not a demonstration of
-- a failing application.
CREATE TABLE dbo.ErrorProbe
(
    ProbeID      int IDENTITY(1,1) NOT NULL CONSTRAINT PK_ErrorProbe PRIMARY KEY,
    CaseName     nvarchar(60)   NOT NULL,
    ExpectedNumber int          NOT NULL,
    ActualNumber int            NOT NULL,
    ActualMessage nvarchar(2048) NOT NULL,
    XactState    int            NOT NULL,
    ProbedAt     datetime2(3)   NOT NULL CONSTRAINT DF_ErrorProbe_ProbedAt DEFAULT (SYSUTCDATETIME())
);
GO

DECLARE @Case  nvarchar(60),
        @Expect int,
        @Inner  nvarchar(max);

-- The five classes that a CATCH block has to tell apart.
DECLARE cases TABLE (Ordinal int IDENTITY(1,1) NOT NULL, CaseName nvarchar(60) NOT NULL,
                     Expected int NOT NULL, InnerSql nvarchar(max) NOT NULL);

INSERT INTO cases (CaseName, Expected, InnerSql)
VALUES
 (N'Divide by zero',          8134, N'SELECT @dummy = 1 / 0;'),
 (N'Unknown object',          208, N'SELECT * FROM dbo.ThisTableDoesNotExist;'),
 (N'Column count mismatch',   213, N'INSERT dbo.SalesOrder (SalesOrderID) VALUES (1);'),
 (N'RAISERROR severity 16',  50000, N'RAISERROR (N''deliberate failure'', 16, 1);'),
 (N'RAISERROR severity 11',  50000, N'RAISERROR (N''informational'', 11, 1);');

DECLARE @i int = 1, @Max int = (SELECT COUNT(*) FROM cases), @Msg nvarchar(2048);

WHILE @i <= @Max
BEGIN
    SELECT @Case = CaseName, @Expect = Expected, @Inner = InnerSql
    FROM   cases WHERE Ordinal = @i;

    SET @Msg = N'';

    BEGIN TRY
        EXEC sys.sp_executesql @Inner, N'@dummy int OUTPUT', @dummy = 0 OUTPUT;
    END TRY
    BEGIN CATCH
        SET @Msg = ERROR_MESSAGE();
    END CATCH;

    INSERT INTO dbo.ErrorProbe (CaseName, ExpectedNumber, ActualNumber, ActualMessage, XactState)
    VALUES (@Case, @Expect, CASE WHEN @Msg = N'' THEN 0 ELSE -1 END, @Msg, XACT_STATE());

    SET @i += 1;
END

-- The comparison. ActualNumber is reported as 0 or -1 above because
-- ERROR_NUMBER() is only meaningful inside the CATCH block, so the probe
-- records the message and a flag; a production variant would call a procedure
-- that does the classification from inside the handler.
SELECT  CaseName, ExpectedNumber, ActualNumber, ActualMessage
FROM    dbo.ErrorProbe
ORDER BY ProbeID;
GO

-- The classification itself, written as a function so that the production
-- handler and the test harness use the same code.
CREATE OR ALTER FUNCTION dbo.fn_ClassifyError (@number int, @message nvarchar(2048))
RETURNS nvarchar(60)
AS
BEGIN
    RETURN CASE
               WHEN @number = 0    THEN N'No error'
               WHEN @number = 8134 THEN N'Division by zero'
               WHEN @number = 2627 THEN N'Primary key violation'
               WHEN @number = 2601 THEN N'Unique constraint violation'
               WHEN @number = 547  THEN CASE
                                          WHEN @message LIKE N'%FK[_]%'  THEN N'Foreign key violation'
                                          WHEN @message LIKE N'%CK[_]%'  THEN N'Check constraint violation'
                                          ELSE N'Constraint violation (other)'
                                      END
               WHEN @number = 1205 THEN N'Deadlock victim'
               WHEN @number = 8645 THEN N'Memory grant timeout'
               WHEN @number = 208  THEN N'Invalid object name'
               WHEN @number = 4305 THEN N'Broken log chain'
               WHEN @number = 4335 THEN N'STOPAT outside the backup range'
               WHEN @number >= 50000 THEN N'Application error (RAISERROR or THROW)'
               ELSE N'Unclassified'
           END;
END;
GO

SELECT  dbo.fn_ClassifyError(547,  N'The INSERT statement conflicted with the FOREIGN KEY constraint "FK_SalesOrder_Customer".') AS ForeignKey,
        dbo.fn_ClassifyError(547,  N'The INSERT statement conflicted with the CHECK constraint "CK_SalesOrder_Credit".')      AS CheckConstraint,
        dbo.fn_ClassifyError(1205, N'Deadlock detected.')                                                               AS Deadlock,
        dbo.fn_ClassifyError(50000,N'deliberate failure')                                                               AS AppError;
GO

A.4 Isolation levels and lock compatibility

SQL Server implements READ COMMITTEDREAD COMMITTED with shared locks held to the end of the statement, REPEATABLE READREPEATABLE READ and SERIAL with shared locks held to the end of the transaction, and READ UNCOMMITTEDREAD UNCOMMITTED and SNAP with no shared locks at all. The two lock-taking levels are what the compatibility matrix below describes; the two non-locking levels are outside it.

Shared (S)Update (U)Intent (I)Exclusive (X)
Shared (S)✓✓✓—
Update (U)✓—×—
Intent (I)✓×✓—
Exclusive (X)————

A ✓ is compatibility and a × is conflict. The two entries that surprise people are update against intent and update against update: a scan takes intent locks on the pages it reads, so a row-level update conflicts with a page-level scan intent lock even though no two rows are the same. The UU lock is what makes the read-then-update pattern safe: a scan of a page places update locks on each row, so a second transaction wanting the same row waits rather than reading a value that is about to change.

LevelDirty readNon-repeatable readPhantom readMechanismCost
READ UNCOMMITTEDREAD UNCOMMITTEDYesYesYesNo shared locks; a shared lock holder is skipped rather than waited forCheapest, and the only level that can return a value that is later rolled back. A report that produces wrong totals is worse than a report that is slow
READ COMMITTEDREAD COMMITTEDNoYesYesShared locks held to the end of the statementThe default, and the level most applications actually need
REPEATABLE READREPEATABLE READNoNoYesShared locks held to the end of the transactionA range read inside a transaction holds locks for the whole transaction. The setting is per database and changes every connection
SERIALNoNoNoShared locks plus key-range locks on the ranges readBlocks inserts into the ranges being read, which is what prevents phantoms and what makes it the level behind most escalation cases
SNAPNoNoNoRow versions in tem; readers take no locks and writers do not block themRequires ALLOW_SNAPSH or READ_COMMIT on the database, and the version store must be kept small or it consumes tem
-- The two snapshot settings, and they are different in what they change.
-- READ_COMMITTED_SNAPSHOT changes the meaning of READ COMMITTED: it reads
-- from the row version store instead of taking shared locks, so readers never
-- block writers. It cannot be changed while there is a transaction open on
-- the database.
ALTER DATABASE CURRENT SET READ_COMMITTED_SNAPSHOT ON WITH ROLLBACK IMMEDIATE;
GO

-- ALLOW_SNAPSHOT_ISOLATION permits explicit SNAPSHOT transactions, which
-- readers opt into one connection at a time.
ALTER DATABASE CURRENT SET ALLOW_SNAPSHOT_ISOLATION ON;
GO

SELECT  name, is_read_committed_snapshot_on, is_allow_snapshot_isolation_on,
        snapshot_isolation_state_desc, is_oltp_on
FROM    sys.databases
WHERE   database_id = DB_ID();
GO

-- The version store, which is the cost of the setting above. It lives in
-- tempdb, is cleaned up aggressively by the first query that finds it large,
-- and is the reason a long-running read transaction on a snapshot database is
-- a tempdb incident.
SELECT  DB_NAME(database_id)              AS DatabaseName,
        COUNT_BIG(*)                      AS ActiveVersionChainRows,
        CAST(AVG(CAST(version_chain_length AS bigint)) AS decimal(18,2)) AS AvgChainLength
FROM    sys.dm_tran_version_store
GROUP BY database_id
ORDER BY COUNT_BIG(*) DESC;
GO

-- Explicit SNAPSHOT, and the transaction-level setting it depends on.
SET TRANSACTION ISOLATION LEVEL SNAPSHOT;
GO

BEGIN TRANSACTION;

SELECT  o.SalesOrderID, o.TotalAmount
FROM    dbo.SalesOrder AS o
WHERE   o.CustomerID = 42;

-- Another session can now update and delete these rows. It is not blocked
-- and this reader does not see the change.
COMMIT TRANSACTION;
GO

SET TRANSACTION ISOLATION LEVEL READ COMMITTED;
GO

-- The database-level READ COMMITTED settings that change the meaning of every
-- statement, and therefore the least safe to change after go-live.
SELECT  name,
        is_ansi_nulls_on, is_ansi_padding_on, is_ansi_warnings_on,
        is_arithabort_on, is_concat_null_yields_null_on,
        is_numeric_roundabort_on, is_quoted_identifier_on,
        compatibility_level, collation_name
FROM    sys.databases
WHERE   database_id = DB_ID();
GO

-- Lock modes, for reference. The resource types that matter operationally are
-- KEY (a row or index key), PAGE (a page in a data or index file) and OBJECT
-- (the table itself, taken by an ALTER or a TRUNCATE).
SELECT  tl.request_mode,
        tl.resource_type,
        COUNT_BIG(*)                                        AS LockCount,
        COUNT(DISTINCT tl.request_session_id)               AS Sessions
FROM    sys.dm_tran_locks AS tl
GROUP BY tl.request_mode, tl.resource_type
ORDER BY LockCount DESC;
GO
-- The two-session test that demonstrates every level. Run the reader in one
-- session and the writer in another; the reader's result is the answer.
-- Session 1, the reader:
SET TRANSACTION ISOLATION LEVEL READ UNCOMMITTED;
GO
SELECT  Session    = N'READ UNCOMMITTED',
        ReadValue  = (SELECT TotalAmount FROM dbo.SalesOrder WHERE SalesOrderID = 1001);
GO

SET TRANSACTION ISOLATION LEVEL READ COMMITTED;
GO
SELECT  Session    = N'READ COMMITTED',
        ReadValue  = (SELECT TotalAmount FROM dbo.SalesOrder WHERE SalesOrderID = 1001);
GO

SET TRANSACTION ISOLATION LEVEL REPEATABLE READ;
GO
BEGIN TRANSACTION;
SELECT  Session    = N'REPEATABLE READ, read 1',
        ReadValue  = (SELECT TotalAmount FROM dbo.SalesOrder WHERE SalesOrderID = 1001);
-- ... a writer in session 2 updates the row here, and blocks ...
SELECT  Session    = N'REPEATABLE READ, read 2',
        ReadValue  = (SELECT TotalAmount FROM dbo.SalesOrder WHERE SalesOrderID = 1001);
COMMIT TRANSACTION;
GO

SET TRANSACTION ISOLATION LEVEL SNAPSHOT;
GO
BEGIN TRANSACTION;
SELECT  Session    = N'SNAPSHOT, read 1',
        ReadValue  = (SELECT TotalAmount FROM dbo.SalesOrder WHERE SalesOrderID = 1001);
-- ... a writer in session 2 updates the row here, and does not block ...
SELECT  Session    = N'SNAPSHOT, read 2 (unchanged, the row version)',
        ReadValue  = (SELECT TotalAmount FROM dbo.SalesOrder WHERE SalesOrderID = 1001);
COMMIT TRANSACTION;
GO

SET TRANSACTION ISOLATION LEVEL READ COMMITTED;
GO

-- The block-holding diagnosis, in the form to run while it is happening.
-- A transaction that is waiting in a lock queue for longer than a few
-- seconds is a transaction that will be a deadlock candidate if its
-- lock order disagrees with anybody else's.
SELECT  r.session_id,
        r.status,
        r.command,
        r.wait_type,
        r.wait_time,
        r.wait_resource,
        r.blocking_session_id,
        DATEDIFF(second, r.start_time, GETDATE()) AS RunningSeconds,
        s.login_name,
        s.program_name,
        t.text                                      AS BatchText
FROM    sys.dm_exec_requests AS r
LEFT JOIN sys.dm_exec_sessions AS s ON s.session_id = r.session_id
OUTER APPLY sys.dm_exec_sql_text(r.sql_handle) AS t
WHERE   r.blocking_session_id <> 0
    OR  r.wait_type LIKE N'LCK_M_%'
ORDER BY r.wait_time DESC;
GO

-- Killing the blocker, which is a decision rather than a reflex: KILL WITH
-- ROLLBACK undoes the blocking transaction and error 3903 is raised in the
-- killed session, and the work has to be redone.
-- KILL 57;
GO

-- Reducing the chance of it happening again, which is an index and not a
-- statement. A foreign key column with no index makes the parent table's
-- pages take update locks that conflict with a scan.
SELECT  fk.name                                      AS ForeignKeyName,
        OBJECT_NAME(fk.parent_object_id)            AS ChildTable,
        OBJECT_NAME(fk.referenced_object_id)         AS ParentTable,
        c.name                                        AS ChildColumn,
        ic.key_ordinal,
        CASE WHEN ix.index_id IS NULL THEN N'NO INDEX' ELSE ix.name END AS CoveringIndex
FROM    sys.foreign_keys         AS fk
JOIN    sys.foreign_key_columns  AS fkc ON fkc.constraint_object_id = fk.object_id
JOIN    sys.columns              AS c   ON c.object_id = fkc.parent_object_id
                                       AND c.column_id = fkc.parent_column_id
LEFT JOIN sys.index_columns     AS ic  ON ic.object_id = fkc.parent_object_id
                                       AND ic.column_id = fkc.parent_column_id
                                       AND ic.key_ordinal > 0
LEFT JOIN sys.indexes           AS ix  ON ix.object_id = ic.object_id
                                       AND ix.index_id  = ic.index_id
ORDER BY fk.name, ic.key_ordinal;
GO
-- The two settings, stated as the whole of what each one does. A change to
-- either is a change to every connection that does not override it, which is
-- why both are made in a deployment window and recorded in the baseline.
SELECT  is_read_committed_snapshot_on,
        is_allow_snapshot_isolation_on,
        snapshot_isolation_state_desc,
        snapshot_isolation_state
FROM    sys.databases
WHERE   database_id = DB_ID();
GO

-- Reading a snapshot of a table while a writer updates it, which is the case
-- RCSI exists for. The reader does not block, does not block, and sees the
-- pre-update row image.
-- Session 1 (the reader), after the writer has begun but not committed:
SET TRANSACTION ISOLATION LEVEL READ COMMITTED;
GO
BEGIN TRANSACTION;
SELECT  ReadCommittedValue = (SELECT TotalAmount FROM dbo.SalesOrder WHERE SalesOrderID = 1001);
COMMIT TRANSACTION;
GO

-- The version store, which is the cost of the setting. It is cleaned up by the
-- first query that finds it large, so a version store that is persistently
-- large is a database with long readers and no cleanup pressure.
SELECT  DatabaseName  = DB_NAME(database_id),
        version_store_type_desc,
        version_chain_length,
        COUNT_BIG(*) AS Rows
FROM    sys.dm_tran_version_store
GROUP BY database_id, version_store_type_desc, version_chain_length
ORDER BY Rows DESC;
GO

-- Cleaned up by this, and only by a query that runs against the database.
-- A database that is read continuously and written continuously holds its
-- version store at the size of the longest read transaction.
UPDATE dbo.SalesOrder SET TotalAmount = TotalAmount WHERE SalesOrderID < 0;
GO

-- Setting the isolation level, and what happens when the setting cannot be
-- honoured. SNAPSHOT on a database without ALLOW_SNAPSHOT_ISOLATION fails at
-- the first statement, not at SET time, with error 3952.
SET TRANSACTION ISOLATION LEVEL SNAPSHOT;
GO

BEGIN TRANSACTION;

SELECT  SnapshotValue = (SELECT TotalAmount FROM dbo.SalesOrder WHERE SalesOrderID = 1001);

-- The writer can now update and delete this row. It is not blocked, and this
-- reader does not see the change.
COMMIT TRANSACTION;
GO

SET TRANSACTION ISOLATION LEVEL READ COMMITTED;
GO

-- What the setting actually is for, as a comparison of the blocking behaviour
-- rather than of the isolation semantics. A read that blocks a write is a read
-- that is costing the business money, and RCSI is the switch that stops it.
SELECT  Setting                       = N'READ_COMMITTED_SNAPSHOT',
        Off_BlockingBehaviour         = N'Readers take S locks; a writer waits',
        On_BlockingBehaviour          = N'Readers take row versions; the writer proceeds',
        Cost_Off                      = N'Writer waits for the read to finish',
        Cost_On                       = N'tempdb version store grows with the longest reader'
UNION ALL
SELECT N'ALLOW_SNAPSHOT_ISOLATION', N'Explicit SNAPSHOT still takes S locks',
       N'Explicit SNAPSHOT reads row versions',
       N'Per-connection; only SNAPSHOT connections are affected',
       N'tempdb version store; same mechanism, chosen per connection'
UNION ALL
SELECT N'REPEATABLE READ / SERIALIZABLE (database level)',
       N'Shared locks held to the end of the transaction',
       N'Unchanged; these levels always lock',
       N'Long transactions block writers for their whole duration',
       N'Setting it affects every connection that has not set a level'
ORDER BY Setting;
GO

-- Setting them, and the requirement that catches people: a change to
-- READ_COMMITTED_SNAPSHOT fails with error 3961 if any transaction is open on
-- the database, so WITH ROLLBACK IMMEDIATE is used to make the change possible
-- at the cost of killing other people’s work, which is why it belongs in a
-- window.
ALTER DATABASE CURRENT SET READ_COMMITTED_SNAPSHOT OFF WITH ROLLBACK IMMEDIATE;
GO

ALTER DATABASE CURRENT SET READ_COMMITTED_SNAPSHOT ON WITH ROLLBACK IMMEDIATE;
GO

SELECT  is_read_committed_snapshot_on FROM sys.databases WHERE database_id = DB_ID();
GO

A.5 The DBCC command reference

DB is a mixed namespace: some statements are checks, some are maintenance operations, and a few are global switches with no relationship to the others. The ones listed here are the ones with a scheduled or a diagnostic use.

CommandPurposeOptions that matterWhen to run it
DBCC CHECKDBDBCC CHECKDBFull integrity check: pages, rows, allocation, indexes, catalog, FILES and (with EXTENDE) moreWITH NO_INFOMSGSWITH NO_INFOMSGS, ALL_ER, TAB, PHYSIC, ESTIMA, DATA_PUScheduled, weekly, in a window. Never reactively: a corrupt page is discovered by reading it
DBCC CHECKTABLEDBCC CHECKTABLEThe same check for one table or one partitionAs above, plus the object nameAfter a targeted event, or in a partitioned database to parallelise the work
DBCC CHECKFILEGROUPDBCC CHECKFILEGROUPThe same check for one filegroupAs above, plus the filegroup nameAs above, for a large filegroup
DBCC CHECKALLOCDBCC CHECKALLOCAllocation consistency only: extents, page counts, F and PNO_IN, TAB, SKIP_ALL_CLEAN_AAs part of CHE; rarely on its own
DBCC CHECKCATALOGDBCC CHECKCATALOGSystem catalog consistencyNO_INAs part of CHE
DBCC CHECKCLEANTABLEDBCC CHECKCLEANTABLEOutput of te, nt and im columns, which have no per-row validationTable nameAs part of CHECKDBCHECKDB. Relevant to any database still using the deprecated types
DBCC CHECKIDENTDBCC CHECKIDENTIdentity current value against the maximum in the tableNOREID (the default, safe), REIDE (rewrites every value, rarely correct)After a failed load or a manual reseed, before relying on IDENT_
DBCC SHRINKFILEDBCC SHRINKFILEReturns unused space at the end of a data or log file to the file systemFile name, TARGERarely, and only to prepare for a file removal. Shrinking a file that will grow again causes fragmentation and the log is usually not shrinkable at all
DBCC SHRINKLOGDBCC SHRINKLOGReturns log space, by truncating the active transaction logDatabase name, TARGEOnly in SIM recovery, and only before a file operation. A FU database cannot be shrunk this way at all
DBCC SQLPERFDBCC SQLPERFHistorical performance counters, now largely replaced by the DMVsLOGS, TABS, WAIT, FILEBLOGS is still the shortest way to see log utilisation across all databases
DBCC DROPCLEANBUFFERSDBCC DROPCLEANBUFFERSEmpties the buffer poolNoneA cold-cache measurement on a representative copy. Instance-wide, privileged, and disruptive
DBCC FREEPROCCACHEDBCC FREEPROCCACHEEmpties the plan cacheNoneAlmost never. sp_rec on one object achieves the same purpose without the cost to everything else
DBCC FLUSHPROCINDBDBCC FLUSHPROCINDBEmpties the plan cache for one databaseDatabase nameAs a database-scoped alternative to FREEPR
DBCC OPENTRANDBCC OPENTRANLists the oldest open transaction in each databaseNO_IN, transaction markWhen log_reuse_wait_desc = ACTIVE_TRANSACTIONlog_reuse_wait_desc = ACTIVE_TRANSACTION
DBCC INPUTBUFFERDBCC INPUTBUFFERThe last statement executed in a session, and its parametersSession id, database id, input bufferTo see what a blocked or runaway session is actually doing
DBCC OUTPUTBUFFERDBCC OUTPUTBUFFEREverything a session has output and not yet collectedSession id, database id, output bufferRarely. The fn_trace and DMV equivalents are better
DBCC PAGEDBCC PAGEDumps one page header and, with 00, the raw bytesDatabase id, file id, page id, output optionResearching a specific page, with a support case open
DBCC DATAPAGEDBCC DATAPAGEByte-level page structure, slot arrangement and record contentsAs above, plus the slot numberAs above. Not a substitute for CHE
DBCC INDEXREBUILDDBCC INDEXREBUILDParallel index rebuild; superseded by ALTER INDEX ... REBUILDALTER INDEX ... REBUILDTable name, index nameNot used. Listed because it appears in older maintenance scripts
DBCC BACKUPDBCC BACKUPVerifies a backup by restoring it and running CHE against the resultBackup statement, RESTORESPeriodically, if RESTORE VERIFYONLYRESTORE VERIFYONLY is considered insufficient
DBCC TRACEONDBCC TRACEON / TRACGlobal trace flags; deprecated as a tracing mechanismTrace flag numberOnly for a documented trace flag, and recorded in the instance configuration
-- The integrity check, with the options that make it schedulable. ESTIMATEONLY
-- first, to know what the window has to accommodate.
DBCC CHECKDB (N'SalesDB') WITH ESTIMATEONLY;
GO

DBCC CHECKDB (N'SalesDB') WITH PHYSICAL_ONLY, TABLOCK, ALL_ERRORMSGS, NO_INFOMSGS;
GO

-- Targeted, after a hardware or power event. Partition the work if the
-- database is large enough that one run would not fit the window.
DBCC CHECKTABLE (N'SalesDB.dbo.SalesOrder') WITH PHYSICAL_ONLY, NO_INFOMSGS;
GO

-- The identity check, in its safe form. NOREIDENTIFY reports the discrepancy
-- without changing anything; REIDENTIFY reseeds to the current maximum and is
-- the wrong choice in almost every case.
DBCC CHECKIDENT (N'dbo.SalesOrder') WITH NOREIDENTIFY;
GO

-- Log space across all databases, which is still the shortest form of the
-- question.
DBCC SQLPERF (LOGSPACE);
GO

-- The oldest open transaction, which is the first thing to check when
-- log_reuse_wait_desc is ACTIVE_TRANSACTION.
DBCC OPENTRAN;
GO

-- What a session is doing, when it cannot be asked.
DBCC INPUTBUFFER (57, 0);
GO

-- The page, when a support case asks for it.
SELECT  DB_NAME(database_id) AS DatabaseName, file_id, page_id
FROM    sys.dm_os_page_info
WHERE   database_id = DB_ID(N'SalesDB')
ORDER BY file_id, page_id;
GO

-- Statistics display, which is not a DBCC statement but belongs beside the
-- integrity commands because it is the other half of "is this table sound".
DBCC SHOW_STATISTICS (N'dbo.SalesOrder', N'IX_SalesOrder_CustomerID_OrderDate')
    WITH HISTOGRAM;
GO

-- Space usage per partition, the operational counterpart to CHECKDB.
SELECT  OBJECT_SCHEMA_NAME(p.object_id) + '.' + OBJECT_NAME(p.object_id) AS TableName,
        p.partition_number,
        p.rows,
        p.reserved_page_count * 8                                      AS ReservedKB,
        p.used_page_count * 8                                          AS UsedKB
FROM    sys.dm_db_partition_stats AS p
JOIN    sys.partitions            AS sp ON sp.partition_id = p.partition_id
WHERE   p.database_id = DB_ID()
ORDER BY p.rows DESC;
GO

-- The extended events equivalent of a trace, which is what to put in place
-- of a maintenance trace. Three events, three predicates, one ring buffer,
-- and a session that starts with the instance so that the first incident is
-- captured without anybody having enabled anything.
CREATE EVENT SESSION [Ops_Watch] ON SERVER
ADD EVENT sqlserver.error_reported
(
    WHERE (sqlserver.error_reported.error_number >= 5000
        OR sqlserver.error_reported.error_number IN (1205, 8645, 229, 2627, 547, 3961, 3930))
),
ADD EVENT sqlserver.blocked_process_reported
(
    SET reporting_options = (capture_reports_waiting = 1)
),
ADD EVENT sqlserver.xml_deadlock_report
ADD TARGET package.ring_buffer
(
    SET ring_buffer_target = N'<targets><event_session name="Ops_Watch" session="1"><target name="package_ring_buffer"><setting name="max_memory">64</setting><setting name="max_events">2000</setting></target></event_session></targets>'
)
WITH
(
    MAX_MEMORY           = 64 MB,
    EVENT_RETENTION_MODE = ALLOW_SINGLE_EVENT_LOSS,
    MAX_DISPATCH_LATENCY = 5 SECONDS,
    TRACK_CAUSALITY      = OFF,
    STARTUP_STATE        = ON
);
GO

-- Reading it. The blocked_process_reported event is the one that replaces
-- the trace most often run for blocking, and it carries the blocking
-- statement as well as the blocked one.
SELECT
    t.value('@timestamp', 'datetime2(3)')                            AS EventTime,
    t.value('@name', 'sysname')                                       AS EventName,
    t.value('(data[@name="error_number"]/value)[1]', 'int')           AS ErrorNumber,
    t.value('(data[@name="duration"]/value)[1]', 'bigint')            AS DurationMs,
    t.value('(data[@name="blocked_process_id"]/value)[1]', 'int')     AS BlockedProcessId,
    t.value('(data[@name="blocking_process_id"]/value)[1]', 'int')    AS BlockingProcessId
FROM   sys.dm_xe_session_targets AS st
JOIN   sys.dm_xe_sessions        AS s  ON s.address = st.event_session_address
CROSS APPLY st.target_data.nodes('//RingBufferTarget/event') AS x(t)
WHERE  s.name = N'Ops_Watch'
ORDER BY EventTime DESC;
GO
-- Space usage per file, which is the other half of "is this table sound" and
-- the one that answers a capacity question rather than an integrity one.
SELECT  DB_NAME(fs.database_id)                             AS DatabaseName,
        mf.name                                            AS LogicalName,
        mf.physical_name,
        mf.type_desc,
        CAST(mf.size * 8.0 / 1024 AS decimal(12,1))        AS SizeMB,
        mf.max_size,
        mf.growth,
        mf.is_percent_growth,
        DATEDIFF(day, mf.create_date, GETDATE())           AS AgeDays
FROM    sys.master_files AS mf
JOIN    sys.database_files AS df ON df.file_id = mf.file_id
                             AND df.database_id = mf.database_id
LEFT JOIN sys.dm_io_virtual_file_stats(NULL, mf.file_id) AS fs
       ON fs.database_id = mf.database_id
ORDER BY mf.size DESC;
GO

-- The maintenance job, as one script, so that the schedule and the commands
-- are the same artefact and cannot drift apart. Run it with a runtime gate
-- and an explicit log, and read both afterwards.
CREATE OR ALTER PROCEDURE dbo.usp_NightlyMaintenance
    @MaxRuntimeMinutes int = 60
AS
BEGIN
    SET NOCOUNT ON;
    SET XACT_ABORT ON;

    DECLARE @start datetime2(3) = SYSDATETIME(),
            @log   nvarchar(max) = N'',
            @sql   nvarchar(max);

    -- 1. Statistics, before anything is rebuilt: a rebuild updates them
    --    anyway, and a fresh estimate is what the first query of the morning
    --    will use.
    EXEC sys.sp_updatestats;

    -- 2. Index maintenance, gated on size and fragmentation. The thresholds
    --    are the ones from chapter 11 and are deliberately conservative on
    --    solid-state storage.
    EXEC dbo.usp_MaintainIndexes @MinSizeMB = 1024, @ReorgPercent = 15,
                                @RebuildPercent = 30, @MaxRuntimeMins = @MaxRuntimeMinutes;

    -- 3. Log backups, so the chain does not fall behind while the rest runs.
    SET @sql = STRING_AGG(
                 N'BACKUP LOG ' + QUOTENAME(name) +
                 N' TO DISK = N''D:\Backup\' + name + N'_log.trn''
                   WITH INIT, CHECKSUM, COMPRESSION = ON, NOCHECKPOINT, STATS = 5;',
                 N'' + CHAR(10))
             FROM   sys.databases
             WHERE  database_id > 4
               AND  state_desc = N'ONLINE'
               AND  recovery_model_desc = N'FULL'
               AND  is_read_only = 0;

    IF LEN(@sql) > 0
        EXEC sys.sp_executesql @sql;

    -- 4. The report. A maintenance job that produces no output cannot be
    --    distinguished from one that did nothing.
    SET @log = N'Maintenance completed. ElapsedMs=' +
               CAST(DATEDIFF_BIG(millisecond, @start, SYSDATETIME()) AS nvarchar(20));

    SELECT  StartedAt          = @start,
            FinishedAt         = SYSDATETIME(),
            ElapsedMs          = DATEDIFF_BIG(millisecond, @start, SYSDATETIME()),
            WithinBudget       = CASE WHEN DATEDIFF(minute, @start, SYSDATETIME()) <= @MaxRuntimeMinutes
                                      THEN 1 ELSE 0 END,
            LogReuseOutstanding = (SELECT COUNT_BIG(*) FROM sys.databases
                                   WHERE database_id > 4
                                     AND log_reuse_wait_desc <> N'NOTHING');
END;
GO

EXEC dbo.usp_NightlyMaintenance @MaxRuntimeMinutes = 60;
GO
-- The maintenance commands, as a schedulable block. The two that are run
-- nightly are statistics and the integrity check; the rest are on demand. The
-- runtime gate is the important part: a check that is killed by the end of the
-- window has consumed the window and produced nothing.
DECLARE @start datetime2(3) = SYSDATETIME(),
        @Budget int = 45;

-- 1. Statistics first. A rebuild below updates them anyway, and a fresh
--    estimate is what the first query of the morning will use.
EXEC sys.sp_updatestats;

-- 2. Then the integrity check, one database at a time, so that one large
--    database does not consume the whole window before the others are reached.
DECLARE @db sysname;

DECLARE db_cursor CURSOR LOCAL FAST_FORWARD FOR
    SELECT name FROM sys.databases
    WHERE  database_id > 4
      AND  state_desc = N'ONLINE'
      AND  recovery_model_desc <> N'SNAPSHOT'
      AND  source_database_id IS NULL
      AND  is_read_only = 0;

OPEN db_cursor;
FETCH NEXT FROM db_cursor INTO @db;

WHILE @@FETCH_STATUS = 0
BEGIN
    IF DATEDIFF(minute, @start, SYSDATETIME()) >= @Budget
    BEGIN
        PRINT N'Budget exhausted before ' + @db;
        BREAK;
    END

    EXEC ('DBCC CHECKDB (' + QUOTENAME(@db) + ') WITH PHYSICAL_ONLY, TABLOCK, NO_INFOMSGS;');

    FETCH NEXT FROM db_cursor INTO @db;
END

CLOSE db_cursor;
DEALLOCATE db_cursor;

SELECT  FinishedAt      = SYSDATETIME(),
        ElapsedMinutes  = DATEDIFF(minute, @start, SYSDATETIME()),
        WithinBudget    = CASE WHEN DATEDIFF(minute, @start, SYSDATETIME()) <= @Budget
                               THEN 1 ELSE 0 END;
GO

-- The partitioning form, which is how a large database is made to fit a
-- window: one partition per job, and the same total work spread over the week.
-- A check that has only covered half the data has told you nothing about the
-- other half, so the schedule has to record which partitions were covered.
DECLARE @PartitionNumber int = 1, @MaxPartition int;

SELECT @MaxPartition = MAX(partition_number)
FROM   sys.partitions
WHERE  object_id = OBJECT_ID(N'dbo.SalesOrder');

WHILE @PartitionNumber <= @MaxPartition
BEGIN
    EXEC ('DBCC CHECKPARTITION (SalesDB.dbo.SalesOrder, ' + CAST(@PartitionNumber AS varchar(10))
          + ') WITH PHYSICAL_ONLY, NO_INFOMSGS;');
    SET @PartitionNumber += 1;
END
GO

-- The identity check, which is the one that finds a reseed that went wrong
-- after a failed load. NOREIDENTIFY is the safe form: it reports and changes
-- nothing. REIDENTIFY rewrites the current value to the maximum in the table,
-- which is the correct action exactly when the table is empty or the values are
-- otherwise known to be contiguous, and is data destruction otherwise.
DBCC CHECKIDENT (N'dbo.SalesOrder') WITH NOREIDENTIFY;
GO

-- The page and record, for a support case. A page dump is not a substitute for
-- CHECKDB and is only useful with a case number.
DECLARE @DbId int = DB_ID(N'SalesDB'),
        @FileId int = 1,
        @PageId int = 0;

SELECT  DatabaseName = DB_NAME(@DbId),
        FileId      = @FileId,
        PageId      = @PageId,
        ObjectName  = OBJECT_NAME(EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM sys.dm_db_page_info
                                           WHERE database_id = @DbId
                                             AND file_id     = @FileId
                                             AND page_id     = @PageId)),
        IndexName   = (SELECT INDEX_NAME(EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM sys.dm_db_page_info
                                                   WHERE database_id = @DbId
                                                     AND file_id     = @FileId
                                                     AND page_id     = @PageId)));

DBCC PAGE (@DbId, @FileId, @PageId) WITH DIAGNOSTICS;
GO

-- The one that replaced most of the rest: an Extended Events session that
-- captures what the older maintenance scripts were reaching for, starts with
-- the instance, and is bounded. The predicates are the whole of the value; an
-- event session without a predicate is a performance incident.
CREATE EVENT SESSION [Health_Watch] ON SERVER
ADD EVENT sqlserver.database_file_size_changed
(
    WHERE (sqlserver.database_file_size_changed.file_type = 1)   -- data file
),
ADD EVENT sqlserver.transaction_log_auto_grow
(
    WHERE (sqlserver.transaction_log_auto_grow.duration >= 0)
),
ADD EVENT sqlserver.error_reported
(
    WHERE (sqlserver.error_reported.severity >= 11)
)
ADD TARGET package.event_file
(
    SET filename = N'D:\XEL\Health_Watch',
        max_file_size = 50,
        max_rollover_files = 5
)
WITH
(
    MAX_MEMORY           = 16 MB,
    EVENT_RETENTION_MODE = ALLOW_SINGLE_EVENT_LOSS,
    MAX_DISPATCH_LATENCY = 30 SECONDS,
    STARTUP_STATE        = ON
);
GO

ALTER EVENT SESSION [Health_Watch] ON SERVER STATE = START;
GO

-- Reading it, once, across the rolled files. This is the evidence that a log
-- file grew automatically, which is a configuration fault rather than a
-- performance problem and is invisible in every counter except this one.
SELECT
    t.value('@timestamp', 'datetime2(3)')                            AS EventTime,
    t.value('@name', 'sysname')                                       AS EventName,
    t.value('(data[@name="duration"]/value)[1]', 'bigint')            AS DurationMs,
    t.value('(data[@name="file_name"]/value)[1]', 'nvarchar(260)')    AS FileName,
    t.value('(data[@name="new_file_size"]/value)[1]', 'bigint')       AS NewSizeBytes,
    t.value('(data[@name="error_number"]/value)[1]', 'int')           AS ErrorNumber,
    t.value('(data[@name="message"]/value)[1]', 'nvarchar(2048)')     AS MessageText
FROM   sys.fn_xe_file_target_read_file(N'D:\XEL\Health_Watch*.xel',
                                       N'D:\XEL\Health_Watch.xel',
                                       N'D:\XEL\Health_Watch*.xel',
                                       null) AS x
CROSS APPLY x.event_data.nodes('//event') AS e(t)
ORDER BY EventTime DESC;
GO

ALTER EVENT SESSION [Health_Watch] ON SERVER STATE = STOP;
GO

-- Reading the auto-grow events directly from the always-on default trace,
-- which needs no session and no file, for the one question this section is
-- most often opened for.
SELECT  StartTime,
        EventClass,
        TextData   AS FileName,
        IntegerData AS SizeInMB,
        Duration   AS GrowMilliseconds
FROM    fn_trace_gettable(
            (SELECT CAST(value AS nvarchar(260)) FROM sys.configurations
             WHERE name = N'log_file_default'),
            DEFAULT)
WHERE   EventClass = 94        -- FileGrowth
ORDER BY StartTime DESC;
GO

-- Log space, one line, every database. The shortest answer to "is anything
-- about to fill a volume", and still the first command to run when a database
-- has stopped.
DBCC SQLPERF (LOGSPACE);
GO

A.6 The 2025 feature matrix, upgrade guidance and documentation

The product state as of SQL Server 2025 (17.x), generally available on 18 November 2025 with build 17.0.1000.7, and as of cumulative update 9 (17.0.5005.3) of 15 September 2026. The table separates what may be built on from what may not, and the upgrade guidance names the changes that are the reason a rehearsal is mandatory.

FeatureVersionStatus in 2025Production-readyNote
Always On availability groups, read-scale replicas, contained availability2012, extended 2022Supported✓The recommended high-availability technology; database mirroring is the deprecated alternative
Temporal tables, ledger tables, graph tables and MA2016 to 2022Supported✓Ledger adds tamper-evidence to a history table
JSON functions and indexes2016Supported✓Projection into typed columns is still the performance answer
Change Data Capture, Change Tracking2008, 2008 R2Supported✓Neither is an audit store; see 13.2
Service Broker2005Supported✓In-database, asynchronous only in the sense of the queue; see 13.3
Memory-optimized tables, MEMORY_O indexes2014Supported✓Requires a snapshot isolation setting; see 13.6
Accelerated Database Recovery2019Supported✓A persistent version store; large log truncation and fast recovery
UTF-8 collations, broader UTF-8 support2019, extended 2025Supported✓Wider support is a 2025 addition; a UTF-8 collation changes the byte representation of every non-ASCII value
ZSTD backup compression2025Supported✓New compression algorithm; version-gate the COMPR keyword. See 12.6
Non-blocking lock and latch improvements (optional)2025Supported, opt-in✓Enabled by a trace flag; test the workload before and after
PREVIEW_ database scoped configuration2025Supported as a mechanism—The gate itself, not a feature; see below
Native vec type, CREATE VECTOR INDEXCREATE VECTOR INDEX, VECTOR2025Preview, gated by PREVIEW_—Not production-ready in 2025. Semantics, storage format and indexes may change before general availability
Fuzzy string matching: EDIT_D, EDIT_DISTANC, JARO_WINKL, JARO_WINKLE2025Preview, gated—Not production-ready in 2025
Half-precision vector storage2025Preview, gated—Not production-ready in 2025
Change event streaming to Azure Event Hubs2025Preview, gated—Not production-ready in 2025; do not build a pipeline on it
Database Mirroring2005Deprecated—Use Always On. See 12.5
PolyBase and external tables2016Deprecated; a breaking-change area in 2025—Retire from new designs. See 13.3
Data Quality Services2012Discontinued in 2025—No replacement in the product; plan the removal
te, nt, im1980sDeprecated types—Migrate to the m variants. See 13.5
Traces and SQL ProfilerSQL 2000Deprecated—Extended Events; see 11.3
Hot add CPU2008Deprecated in 2025—Remove from capacity plans
Express with Advanced Services2008Discontinued in 2025—Migrate to Express or Standard
SUSE Linux Enterprise Server—Unsupported from 2025—Red Hat Enterprise Linux or an Azure SQL platform
-- The preview gate. PREVIEW_FEATURES is a database scoped configuration, so
-- it is set per database and it is OFF by default. A script that enables a
-- preview feature and a deployment that enables the gate are the same
-- decision, and both must be recorded.
SELECT  name, value, value_for_secondary, description
FROM    sys.database_scoped_configurations
WHERE   name = N'PREVIEW_FEATURES';
GO

ALTER DATABASE CURRENT SET PREVIEW_FEATURES = ON;
GO

-- A version guard that stops a preview script reaching a production instance.
-- The check is the engine's own version, not a variable someone remembers to
-- set.
IF CAST(SERVERPROPERTY('ProductMajorVersion') AS int) < 17
BEGIN
    RAISERROR (N'This statement requires SQL Server 2025 or later.', 16, 1);
    RETURN;
END;
GO

-- The same for ZSTD compression, which fails to parse on 2022 rather than
-- being ignored, so the branch has to exist in the script.
IF CAST(SERVERPROPERTY('ProductMajorVersion') AS int) >= 17
    EXEC sys.sp_executesql
         N'BACKUP DATABASE dbo.SalesDB TO DISK = N''D:\Backup\c.bak''
               WITH INIT, COMPRESSION = ZSTD, CHECKSUM, STATS = 10;';
ELSE
    EXEC sys.sp_executesql
         N'BACKUP DATABASE dbo.SalesDB TO DISK = N''D:\Backup\c.bak''
               WITH INIT, COMPRESSION = ON, CHECKSUM, STATS = 10;';
GO

-- The state of the preview features in a database, and whether anything in the
-- schema depends on one. A database containing a vector column cannot be
-- restored onto a build that does not have the type.
SELECT  s.name                                            AS SchemaName,
        t.name                                            AS TableName,
        ty.name                                           AS ColumnType,
        c.name                                            AS ColumnName
FROM    sys.columns  AS c
JOIN    sys.tables   AS t  ON t.object_id = c.object_id
JOIN    sys.schemas  AS s  ON s.schema_id = t.schema_id
JOIN    sys.types    AS ty ON ty.user_type_id = c.user_type_id
WHERE   ty.name IN (N'vector')
ORDER BY s.name, t.name, c.column_id;
GO
ApproachHow it is doneUse it whenRisk
In-place upgradeRun setup against the existing instance, which upgrades the binaries in place and keeps the data files, the logins and the configurationSingle instance, an accepted maintenance window, and a tested upgrade path from the exact buildAll of the breaking changes apply at once, and the rollback is a restore of everything taken before the window opened. The service is down for the whole window
Side-by-sideInstall 2025 as a new instance, restore or migrate the databases, cut the applications over, and decommission the old instanceAny instance where the rollback has to be fast, where the platform changes, or where the licensing or the edition changesTwo instances during the migration, a data-movement step, and the need to keep logins, permissions, agent jobs and linked servers in step manually or with a script
Azure SQL migrationAssess with Data Migration Assessment, migrate to Azure SQL Database, Managed Instance or an Azure virtual machineThe target is a managed platform, or the licensing or the operations model has changedFeature parity is not complete: xp_cm, cross-database queries, some trace features and some CLR configurations do not exist. The assessment tool is the only reliable answer

The four breaking-change areas in SQL Server 2025. An upgrade plan that touches linked servers, replication, log shipping or PolyBase must be validated against the 2025 release notes before the window opens, and the validation is a rehearsal on a restored copy rather than a reading of the notes. These are the areas where behaviour changed rather than where a new option appeared, which is why they are not visible until the workload runs. Discontinued is also a change: DQS and Express with Advanced Services do not exist in 2025, and SUSE Linux is not a supported platform, so an instance on it cannot be upgraded in place and must move. Deprecated is not yet a change: database mirroring, PolyBase and the deprecated types still work in 2025 and are on the way out.

-- The pre-upgrade inventory, which is the document that decides whether the
-- upgrade is an in-place or a side-by-side. Anything it reports as used must
-- be checked against the release notes before the window opens.
SELECT  Feature                = N'Database mirroring',
        InUse                  = CASE WHEN EXISTS
                                          (SELECT 1 FROM sys.database_mirroring
                                           WHERE mirroring_guid IS NOT NULL)
                                        THEN 1 ELSE 0 END
UNION ALL
SELECT N'PolyBase / external tables',
       CASE WHEN EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM sys.external_tables) THEN 1 ELSE 0 END
UNION ALL
SELECT N'Data Quality Services schemas present',
       CASE WHEN EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM sys.schemas
                          WHERE name IN (N'DQS', N'TDQC')) THEN 1 ELSE 0 END
UNION ALL
SELECT N'Linked servers',
       CASE WHEN EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM sys.servers WHERE is_linked = 1) THEN 1 ELSE 0 END
UNION ALL
SELECT N'Replication (publisher or distributor)',
       CASE WHEN EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM sys.databases WHERE is_published = 1 OR is_subscribed = 1
                                                  OR is_distributor = 1) THEN 1 ELSE 0 END
UNION ALL
SELECT N'Deprecated LOB types (text, ntext, image)',
       CASE WHEN EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM sys.columns WHERE system_type_id IN (35, 99, 34)) THEN 1 ELSE 0 END
UNION ALL
SELECT N'Cross-database queries',
       CASE WHEN EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM sys.sql_modules
                          WHERE definition LIKE N'%.dbo.%') THEN 1 ELSE 0 END
UNION ALL
SELECT N'xp_cmdshell enabled',
       CASE WHEN EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM sys.configurations WHERE name = N'xp_cmdshell' AND value = 1)
            THEN 1 ELSE 0 END
UNION ALL
SELECT N'Trace sessions still running',
       CASE WHEN EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM sys.traces WHERE is_active = 1) THEN 1 ELSE 0 END
ORDER BY InUse DESC, Feature;
GO

-- The edition comparison that a licensed estate needs before an upgrade,
-- because the capacity ceilings moved in 2025 and two features were removed.
-- This is a static reference table, not a query: the values are licensing
-- facts, not something an instance can report about itself.
SELECT  Feature                    = N'Express max database size',
        Before2025                 = N'50 GB (Express with Advanced Services)',
        In2025                     = N'50 GB (Express); Express with Advanced Services discontinued'
UNION ALL
SELECT N'Standard core/socket cap', N'4 sockets / 32 cores', N'4 sockets / 32 cores, buffer pool capped at 256 GB'
UNION ALL
SELECT N'Resource Governor',       N'Enterprise only', N'Enterprise and Standard'
UNION ALL
SELECT N'Hot add CPU',             N'Supported', N'Deprecated'
UNION ALL
SELECT N'SUSE Linux',              N'Supported', N'Not a supported platform'
UNION ALL
SELECT N'Data Quality Services',   N'Supported', N'Discontinued';
GO

-- The compatibility level, which is what actually governs the query optimiser
-- in the upgraded database. A database is left at its previous level on
-- upgrade and raised separately, so the first performance regression after an
-- upgrade is usually the compatibility level and not the version.
SELECT  name, compatibility_level, compatibility_level_desc, collation_name
FROM    sys.databases
WHERE   database_id > 4
ORDER BY name;
GO

ALTER DATABASE [SalesDB] SET COMPATIBILITY_LEVEL = 170;
GO

-- Query Store, which is the tool for comparing the old optimiser against the
-- new one without changing anything. On by default from 2022; the level below
-- confirms it, and the forced-plan facility is how a regression is held
-- steady while the queries are corrected.
SELECT  q.query_id,
        q.query_text,
        p.plan_id,
        p.is_current_plan,
        rs.count_executions,
        rs.avg_duration,
        rs.avg_logical_io_reads
FROM    sys.query_store_query   AS q
JOIN    sys.query_store_plan    AS p ON p.query_id = q.query_id
JOIN    sys.query_store_runtime_stats AS rs ON rs.plan_id = p.plan_id
ORDER BY rs.avg_duration DESC;
GO
ResourceWhat it is authoritative for
Microsoft Learn: SQL Server documentationThe normative reference for every statement, view, error number and option in this guide. The URL pattern is learn.microsoft.com/sql/sql-server/learn.microsoft.com/sql/sql-server/ followed by the feature name
SQL Server 2025 release notes and the list of fixed bugsThe definitive source for what a cumulative update changes and removes. Read the “what’s new” and the breaking-changes sections for linked servers, replication, log shipping and PolyBase before every upgrade
SQL Server build numbers and the CU/GDR listEstablishes which build is deployed and which is current. GA is 17.0.1000.7 of 18 November 2025; CU9 is 17.0.5005.3 of 15 September 2026
Support lifecycle dates, by versionExtended support for 2017, 2019, 2022 and 2025, and the order in which they retire
Database Engine error messages (errors 4000 to 4999)The full text and the cause of every error number; the localisable part of the number to meaning mapping
Transact-SQL language referenceGrammar and behaviour for every statement; the compatibility-level table is here and is the reason a query can behave differently on the same version
Database Engine DMV and function referenceColumn definitions for every view in A.2, including the ones whose names differ from the obvious guess
Always On availability groups documentationThe listener, routing, failover modes, the cluster prerequisites, and the permissions each statement requires
Preview features announcement and the PREVIEW_ referenceWhat is in preview in 2025, what is not production-ready, and the contractual expectation that a preview feature may change
Data Migration Assessment for Azure SQLFeature parity, which is the only reliable answer to “can this move”
-- The cross-platform inventory, which is the part of a migration that cannot
-- be skipped. The list of what a database uses is short; the list of what
-- breaks when it moves to a different platform is the same list.
SELECT  Feature = N'Cross-database three-part names',
        Found   = CASE WHEN EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM sys.sql_modules
                                     WHERE definition LIKE N'%.dbo.%') THEN 1 ELSE 0 END
UNION ALL
SELECT N'Extended stored procedures (xp_cmdshell and the rest)',
       CASE WHEN EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM sys.sql_modules
                          WHERE definition LIKE N'%xp[_]%') THEN 1 ELSE 0 END
UNION ALL
SELECT N'Linked servers',
       CASE WHEN EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM sys.servers WHERE is_linked = 1) THEN 1 ELSE 0 END
UNION ALL
SELECT N'CLR assemblies',
       CASE WHEN EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM sys.assemblies) THEN 1 ELSE 0 END
UNION ALL
SELECT N'SQL Server Agent jobs',
       CASE WHEN EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM msdb.dbo.sysjobs) THEN 1 ELSE 0 END
UNION ALL
SELECT N'Replication (publisher, subscriber or distributor)',
       CASE WHEN EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM sys.databases
                          WHERE is_published = 1 OR is_subscribed = 1 OR is_distributor = 1)
            THEN 1 ELSE 0 END
UNION ALL
SELECT N'Service Broker objects',
       CASE WHEN EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM sys.service_queues) THEN 1 ELSE 0 END
UNION ALL
SELECT N'Availability groups',
       CASE WHEN EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM sys.availability_groups) THEN 1 ELSE 0 END
UNION ALL
SELECT N'PolyBase external tables',
       CASE WHEN EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM sys.external_tables) THEN 1 ELSE 0 END
UNION ALL
SELECT N'Temporal or ledger tables',
       CASE WHEN EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM sys.tables WHERE temporal_type <> 0) THEN 1 ELSE 0 END
UNION ALL
SELECT N'Graph tables',
       CASE WHEN EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM sys.tables WHERE is_graph = 1) THEN 1 ELSE 0 END
UNION ALL
SELECT N'Memory-optimized tables',
       CASE WHEN EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM sys.tables WHERE is_memory_optimized = 1) THEN 1 ELSE 0 END
UNION ALL
SELECT N'Spatial columns',
       CASE WHEN EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM sys.columns
                          WHERE system_type_id IN (240, 241)) THEN 1 ELSE 0 END
UNION ALL
SELECT N'TDE or column encryption keys',
       CASE WHEN EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM sys.dm_database_encryption_keys) THEN 1 ELSE 0 END
UNION ALL
SELECT N'Deprecated LOB types',
       CASE WHEN EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM sys.columns
                          WHERE system_type_id IN (35, 99, 34)) THEN 1 ELSE 0 END
UNION ALL
SELECT N'xp_cmdshell enabled',
       CASE WHEN EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM sys.configurations
                          WHERE name = N'xp_cmdshell' AND value = 1) THEN 1 ELSE 0 END
ORDER BY Found DESC, Feature;
GO

-- The Data Migration Assessment, which is the only tool that answers the
-- question authoritatively rather than from a list. It runs against a live
-- database for a bounded period and produces a report per feature.
SELECT  assessment_type, feature_name, compatibility_level, enabled
FROM    sys.dm_db_assessment_*
ORDER BY assessment_type, feature_name;
GO

-- The version and edition facts that a licensed estate needs, as a single
-- query against the current instance so that the values in the table above
-- can be checked rather than remembered.
SELECT  MajorRelease = CASE CAST(SERVERPROPERTY('ProductMajorVersion') AS int)
                          WHEN 17 THEN N'SQL Server 2025'
                          WHEN 16 THEN N'SQL Server 2022'
                          WHEN 15 THEN N'SQL Server 2019'
                          WHEN 14 THEN N'SQL Server 2017'
                          ELSE N'Unknown'
                      END,
        Build          = CAST(SERVERPROPERTY('ProductVersion') AS nvarchar(50)),
        CumulativeUpdate = CAST(SERVERPROPERTY('ProductLevel') AS nvarchar(50)),
        Edition        = CAST(SERVERPROPERTY('Edition') AS nvarchar(100)),
        IsAzure        = CAST(SERVERPROPERTY('IsAzure') AS int),
        IsIntegrated   = CAST(SERVERPROPERTY('IsIntegratedSecurityOnly') AS int);
GO

-- The 2025 additions, identified on the instance rather than taken from a
-- note. Each of these is a query that returns rows only on a build that has
-- the feature, which is how a script detects a capability without a version
-- string.
SELECT Capability = N'ZSTD backup compression',
        Present    = CASE WHEN CAST(SERVERPROPERTY('ProductMajorVersion') AS int) >= 17
                          AND OBJECT_ID(N'sys.configurations') IS NOT NULL THEN 1 ELSE 0 END
UNION ALL
SELECT N'PREVIEW_FEATURES database scoped configuration',
       CASE WHEN EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM sys.database_scoped_configurations
                          WHERE name = N'PREVIEW_FEATURES') THEN 1 ELSE 0 END
UNION ALL
SELECT N'Native vector type',
       CASE WHEN EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM sys.types WHERE name = N'vector') THEN 1 ELSE 0 END
UNION ALL
SELECT N'Fuzzy string matching functions',
       CASE WHEN EXISTS (SELECT 1 sys.all_objects WHERE name = N'EDIT_DISTANCE_SIMILARITY')
            THEN 1 ELSE 0 END
UNION ALL
SELECT N'Graph MATCH (SHOREST_PATH)',
       CASE WHEN EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM sys.tables WHERE is_graph = 1) THEN 1 ELSE 0 END
UNION ALL
SELECT N'Query Store forced plans',
       CASE WHEN EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM sys.database_scoped_configurations
                          WHERE name LIKE N'QUERY_STORE%') THEN 1 ELSE 0 END
ORDER BY Capability;
GO

-- The isolation between production-ready and preview, enforced in a script
-- rather than in a convention. A deployment that references a preview feature
-- is refused on an instance that has not opted in, and a report of what is
-- using the gate is what a review reads.
SELECT  DatabaseName  = d.name,
        PreviewEnabled = ds.value,
        PreviewUsers   = (SELECT COUNT_BIG(*) FROM sys.tables t
                          WHERE t.temporal_type = 0)
UNION ALL
SELECT N'---- vector columns found ----', NULL, COUNT_BIG(*)
FROM   sys.columns c
JOIN   sys.types   t ON t.user_type_id = c.user_type_id
WHERE  t.name = N'vector'
HAVING COUNT_BIG(*) > 0
ORDER BY DatabaseName;
GO

-- Removing a preview feature from a database, which is a schema operation and
-- not a setting change: the objects that use it have to go first.
SELECT  ObjectName = OBJECT_SCHEMA_NAME(object_id) + '.' + name,
        ObjectType = type_desc
FROM    sys.objects
WHERE   object_id IN (SELECT object_id FROM sys.columns c
                      JOIN sys.types t ON t.user_type_id = c.user_type_id
                      WHERE t.name = N'vector');
GO

ALTER DATABASE CURRENT SET PREVIEW_FEATURES = OFF;
GO

Appendix Exercises

  1. Write a script that creates the whole reference schema from section 3.2, with every table, constraint and index, and run it twice. Record the output of the second run and confirm that it produces no error and no change.
  2. From A.1, produce the DDL for a table with a nvar column that starts as nvarchar(50)nvarchar(50) and is widened to nvarchar(200)nvarchar(200). Run it and confirm from sys.dm_db_pa whether the rows moved.
  3. From A.1, write a ME against the reference schema with all three WH clauses, run it with a deliberately incomplete source, and record what the WHEN NOT MATCHED BY SOURCEWHEN NOT MATCHED BY SOURCE clause did. Then remove the clause and repeat.
  4. Run the A.2 triage script while a session is deliberately blocked, and record the blocking_, the wait and the two batch texts. Then run the sys.dm_t query and record the resource type and mode of the blocking lock.
  5. Using A.3, deliberately trigger errors 2627, 547, 209 and 8134, and record the number and the message for each. Then write a CA block that distinguishes the four without parsing the message text.
  6. Using A.4, set each of the five isolation levels in turn on a connection and run a two-session test: session A begins a transaction and updates a row; session B reads it at each level, then A rolls back. Record what B saw at each level and explain the mechanism for each result.
  7. Turn on READ_COMMIT on the reference database and repeat exercise 6, recording the differences in blocking and in the values read. Then query sys.dm_tran_ from a long-running reader.
  8. Run DBCC CHECKDBDBCC CHECKDB with ESTIMA on every user database and record the percentages. Then run DBCC SQLPERF(LOGSPACE)DBCC SQLPERF(LOGSPACE) and record the log utilisation, and compare the two approaches to answering “how much of this is really being used”.
  9. Open a transaction in one session, run DBCC OPENTANDBCC OPENTAN from another, and record the output. Then close the transaction and repeat.
  10. From A.6, write the query that reports every database on the instance together with its compatibility level, and identify the ones that would not receive the new optimiser after a 2025 upgrade until the level is raised separately.
  11. Run the pre-upgrade inventory from A.6 against the reference database and record every feature reported as in use. For each, name the change in the matrix that affects it and the check that would be added to a rehearsal plan.
  12. Query sys.database_scop for PREVIEW_ and record its value. Write the version guard from A.6 and confirm that it prevents the statement from running on a build below 2025.
  13. Write a table mapping every DMV in A.2 to the section of the guide that uses it, and confirm that no view in the list is unused. Any view in the list that is not used in the text is a candidate for removal from the list.
